# IBM 7030 Performance Characteristics, Volume 2 (March 8, 1961) -- the
# per-instruction timing atlas, transcribed.
#
#   docs/7030_Performance_Characteristics_Vol2_Mar61.pdf, PDF pages 37-189.
#   33 of the 139 table pages are transcribed here; the #KIND lines below say
#   which, and which remain.
#   Reader: tests/atlas_oracle.py (load_atlas).  Self-check:
#   tests/check_atlas_data.py.  Prose: docs/atlas_oracle.md.
#
# EVERY CELL BELOW WAS READ FROM THE PAGE IMAGE (pdftoppm -r 300 -gray,
# viewed at half size, doubtful cells re-viewed as a full-resolution crop),
# on 2026-11-15.  Nothing was taken from docs/ocr/ or from the PDF's text
# layer.  The sheets mirror the printed pages: one line per printed row,
# cells in printed column order, so a re-reader can lay a sheet beside its
# page.
#
# UNIT.  Every number is MICROSECONDS PER INSTRUCTION, as printed.  PDF 37-38
# (printed 34, 34A): GENER times a thousand copies of one instruction closed
# by a count branch and run ten times, and "the measured elapsed time is
# multiplied by 100 to produce the execution time for 1 million
# instructions; the seconds now indicate integer micro-seconds".  "The last
# digit in each number may be in error by one unit."  One model clock is
# 0.3 us (docs/clock_unit.md), so cycles = us / 0.3.
#
# GRAMMAR.  Fields are separated by TABs.
#   PAGE  pdf=N  printed=N  class=C     starts a page; printed is the page's
#                                       own footer/header number, as read
#   HEAD  text                          the printed heading, verbatim
#   INSTR mnemonic                      the instruction the page times
#   COND  key=value ...                 the heading's conditions, normalised
#   COLS  key ...                       every printed column, left to right
#   SHOWN key ...                       the columns the rows below list; a
#                                       printed column not listed here is
#                                       BLANK ON EVERY ROW of the page
#   BLOCK caption                       a captioned group of rows ("-" for a
#                                       group the page prints no caption for)
#   NOTE  text                          a remark, attached to the page
#   TALLY rule exact near far           the redundancy cross-check counts
#                                       observed for this page (see
#                                       check_atlas_data.py).  Most "far"
#                                       pairs differ for real; they were
#                                       reviewed as grouped lists and any
#                                       that broke its page's pattern was
#                                       re-read on the image (the NOTEs
#                                       name those cells)
#   SUM   n total                       count and sum of the page's numeric
#                                       cells, a guard on the FILE (not on
#                                       the reading)
#   anything else                       a data row: label, then one cell per
#                                       SHOWN column
# CELLS.
#   4.20      a printed number, kept as the printed string
#   -         the cell is blank on the page
#   *24*      the page prints an indicator number in asterisks instead of a
#             time (PDF 113-114: an indicator came on and invalidates it)
#   4.20?     an uncertain reading; the page's NOTE says what the doubt is
#   ~         ink present, not legible
#   a trailing !PF etc. on a row is a printed margin mark
#
# PAGE NUMBERS.  Cite by PDF page.  On the landscape atlas pages
# printed = PDF - 7 (PDF 120 prints 113; PDF 109 prints 102).
#
# COLUMN KEYS, with the printed headings they stand for.
#  class=VFL  (group NO INDEXING then group INDEXING, five columns each)
#    ni_ext ni_ext_wbx ni_ibox ni_int ni_int_wbx
#    ix_ext ix_ext_wbx ix_ibox ix_int ix_int_wbx
#      = EXTERNL OPERAND | EXTERNL WB XOVR | I-BOX OPERAND | INTERNL OPERAND |
#        INTERNL WB XOVR
#  class=FP   (group NO INDEXING then group INDEXING, three columns each)
#    ni_int ni_ext ni_ibox ix_int ix_ext ix_ibox
#      = INTERNAL OPERAND | EXTERNAL OPERAND | I-BOX OPERAND
#  class=IBOX (PDF 109)
#    ext_direct ext_effective int_direct int_effective idx_direct
#    idx_effective immediate
#      = "EXTERNAL OPERAND**INTERNAL OPERAND ** INDEX OPERAND**IMMEDIATE" over
#        "DIRECT EFFECTIVE DIRECT EFFECTIVE DIRECT EFFECTIVE"
#  class=BRANCH (PDF 112)
#    succ_consec unsucc_consec succ_noncon unsucc_noncon   (NO INDEXING)
#    ix_succ_consec ix_unsucc_consec ix_succ_noncon ix_unsucc_noncon
#                                                (BRANCH ADDRESS INDEX)
#      = SUCCES CONSEC | UNSUCC CONSEC | SUCCES NONCON | UNSUCC NONCON
#  class=TRANSMIT (PDF 106, 107)
#    ext_ext ext_int ext_ibox int_int int_ext int_ibox ibox_ibox ibox_ext
#    ibox_int   = EXTERNAL TO EXTERNAL | EXTERNAL TO INTERNAL | ... as keyed
#  class=ENV (PDF 104)
#    quoted one two three four five six seven eight
#      = the figure in the row's caption, then ONE ... EIGHT
#  class=IBOX2 (PDF 110): keys given on the page's own COLS lines
#  PDF 138, 139 (CV, DCV) head the first column of each group ACCUM. OPERAND:
#    ni_accum, ix_accum
#
# THE AUTHORS' OWN FLAGS (docs/atlas_reading_guide.md has the quotations):
#  * "indexing" columns index by a register CONTAINING ZERO (PDF 113);
#  * "forwarding" means IDENTICAL OPERAND ADDRESS (PDF 41, 115);
#  * "to memory" pages are WORST CASE, LAAR queuing (PDF 41);
#  * divide pages divide by one and then by ZERO (PDF 41, 113-115);
#  * floating-point sequences drift: 1 + 1 + 1 ... ten thousand times (PDF 40).

# COVERAGE.  One #KIND line for every PDF page 37-189: page or range, status,
# kind, and the page's heading as read from the image.  status is
#   transcribed   a sheet for it is in this file
#   other         not a table of instruction times (prose, a flow chart, a dump)
#   remaining     an atlas page of times NOT yet transcribed; its heading was
#                 read from the image to index it, its figures were not
# check_atlas_data.py requires these to be total and to agree with the sheets.
#KIND	37-39	other	text	preface; INSTRUCTION SEQUENCE GENERATION AND TIMING (GENER) and its flow chart; printed 34, 34A, 34B
#KIND	40-41	other	text	FLOATING POINT TIMING method and commentary; printed 34C, 34D

HEAD	FLOATING POINT ENVIRONMENTAL TEST
COLS	quoted	one	two	three	four	five	six	seven	eight
SHOWN	quoted	one	two	three	four	five	six	seven	eight
NOTE	A different kind of table (PDF 102-103, printed 95-96): each figure is the AVERAGE instruction time of a group made of one subject instruction (the row) followed by n environment instructions (the column, ONE to EIGHT), all on the same external operand.  The subject's own time is to be computed, t_A = (n+1) t_AV - n t_B, with an error bound of (2n+1) x 0.01 us.  The columns are NOT times of the row's instruction.
NOTE	The column 'quoted' is the figure each row prints in its caption, e.g. "LX (5.73 IN A LX ENVIRONMENT)": the instruction's time in a sequence of itself, quoted from the instruction's own page.  check_atlas_data.py compares the index-arithmetic ones with PDF 109.
NOTE	The two block captions are printed with a gap where the environment's time belongs and the figure is written in by hand: "FOLLOWED BY +(U) OPS AT 2.41 EACH" and "FOLLOWED BY *(U) OPS AT 2.71 [mu]s EACH".  PDF 102 prints the same two figures in type ("2.41 us for +(u) (according to p. 66) and close to 2.71 for *(u) (according to p. 76)").  The block captions below carry the handwritten figures.
